## Milestone 8.8 Complete the Wasp kinetic skills completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.8 | Kinetic Repulse and Kinetic Blast resolve their complete printed text, discard rider included, composing 6.4's batch Push exactly as p.18 prints it. **The count and the distance are now declared separately, because a node has one policy and the card says two things.** "Push up to 2 enemy units adjacent to you 3 spaces" puts the `up to` over the units and states the spaces exactly, so `AdmittedDistances.ForDeclared` takes the least distance a card prints beside the declared one - Kinetic Blast's "3 or 4 spaces" is the first verified distance written as a choice - and the batch declares a maximum number of targets. A declaration naming a maximum makes which units are pushed the chooser's decision; one naming none is p.18's plain batch over every adjacent object, which every declaration before this milestone meant and still means. Both Push declarations moved to **revision 3** together because both resolve through one routine, and only the shared distance reading moved to both: the count bound and the target relation are the batch's, since a named push is handed one object by a selection node that has already answered both. `TargetQuery.StandsInRelationTo` opens the first arm of p.19's relationship vocabulary for "enemy units", reusing `SourceTeamRelation` rather than minting a fourth team enum, and reads a hero's side from the match and a minion's from its own team while leaving a token on nobody's. **The rider is one `Conditional` per pushed hero over a fact the push published.** The batch publishes a stop cause per hero it moved - whether an obstacle stopped it and which hero it was, in the target set's own order - and each rider reads the boolean through 8.5's predicate and hands the same hero to 7.1a's in-hand discard under `if able`, so the stopped hero chooses from their own hand per p.10's worked example and an empty hand gives nothing without stopping the action. Two pairs because the card pushes up to two; a card pushing more and reading the fact for all of them needs a third name declared, which is visible at content load rather than silently dropped. **The batch became the first capability in this build to ask two questions**, which p.18 requires - "you choose the distance and then push all those units" settles the distance over a set that is already named. `EffectCapabilityContext.AnswersAlreadyGiven` carries what a node has already been told, projected from the resolution's own accepted choices, so no new persisted field exists; `Execute` reads them too, which is what lets snapshot restore re-ask a handler what it is offering and get the question the pause was written on. **Ledger question W-04 is answered yes on its recorded proposed reading, and needed no model change.** The official map already states p.19's water: every space adjacent to a playable space of `official-single-lane` is itself playable or declared terrain, held by `TerrainObstacleTests.EverySpaceBesideAPlayableOne_IsPlayableOrDeclaredTerrain`, so a unit pushed off the play area meets modelled terrain and the ordinary obstacle rule answers the card. Past that ring the classification still answers "nothing", deliberately, because 6.8e separated off-map from terrain so no Move-Through permission could carry a unit into the water; the card's reading is therefore stated once on `PushPathResult.StoppedByAnObstacle` and admits both arms. Schema **stays v22** - nothing new reaches the wire - while the corpus and the aggregate-invariance digests are re-pinned for two content causes, measured at the byte level in a throwaway worktree at pre-change revision `43cab2e`: no payload changes length, eleven differ in exactly two bytes (both Push revisions `0x02 -> 0x03`), and `verified-hand-setup-v22` differs in those two plus 54 single `'6' -> '8'` bytes as Wasp's content revision moves `wasp-8-6-rev-1` to `wasp-8-8-rev-1` across eighteen definition rows and thirty-six instance references. Validation: dotnet harness 1288/1288 (from 1262), Unity Edit Mode 1479/1479 (from 1453), Play Mode 41/41 unchanged, living-documentation validator green. | `acc5b2b` |

## Milestone 8.9 Complete the Wasp basic cards completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.9 | Magnetic Dagger and Static Barrier resolve their complete printed text in the configured mirror, and every action performed on a basic card records rulebook p.9's supertype. **The two compositions are declarations, not handlers.** Each card's whole text is one mandatory capability node naming an `ActiveEffectDefinition`, and everything the printed sentence says is definition content on that effect - the scope, the radius, the restricted actors, the overlay's arms - so an effect tree with branches would be stating the same rule twice in a second vocabulary. Both run through `ActiveEffectCreationHandler`, which is milestone 6.8's private modification handler lifted out unchanged rather than a second copy of it. **Magnetic Dagger.** Its Attack is p.13's ordinary non-Ranged one - "Target a unit adjacent to you" is the page's own sentence, so the printed target policy narrows nothing and the combat contracts are untouched - and what this milestone composed is the rider hanging on milestone 8.5's seam at p.14's step 7, after the target has defended or been defeated and removed. The `This turn` effect it creates declares two restrictions, p.19's Swap and p.18's Place, because a declaration naming one would drop half the card, and each names `AffectedObjectItself` and `HeroesOpposingSource` - so a pinned unit may not reposition itself, an enemy hero may not reposition it, and Wasp's own team still may, which is the half a blanket prohibition would lose. Crackling Dagger prints the same sentence and the same icon and will compose onto the same definition when its deferral lifts. **Static Barrier.** Its Basic Skill creates the conditional obstacle overlay milestone 8.7 proved with no numbers in it: both printed arms in one declaration, because they are two halves of one boundary rather than two rules, conditioned on heroes opposing the source so Wasp and her teammates walk through their own barrier and a moment with nobody acting closes nothing. **The disk moved onto the definition and became the printed icon.** p.9 gives a card one Range/Radius icon and says card text mentioning radius refers to its value, so `ActiveEffectDefinition.Radius` states it once for both consumers and `ConditionalObstacleOverlay` stopped carrying one; `DeclaredRadius` says whether the number is that icon or a figure the sentence wrote down, which is milestone 7.2b's distinction on the other side of an action boundary. Both basics print the icon and both take a +1 Radius item, so the resolution composes `ItemBonusQuery` at derivation - nothing is stored, and a hero holding the item finds the pinning field at 4 and the barrier at 3. **The affected scope is the first the board decides.** `UnitsInRadius` names units rather than heroes because p.19 does, selects by milestone 8.8's `TargetQuery.StandsInRelationTo` rather than by a second reading of who is whose enemy, and consults no modification set because it is that set's derivation - which costs nothing, since `Counts As` replaces a type and both types it walks are units. Its uniqueness arm is per controller: without it the first Wasp to resolve a Magnetic Dagger would have suppressed the opposing Wasp's as a duplicate. **p.9's supertype, recorded structurally.** `ResolutionStep.AtCheckpoint` refuses `ActionEnded`, so a resolver reaches the completion checkpoint only through `AtActionEnded`, which names the card, the hero and the kind; the turn authority reads `CardClassification.Basic` off the card and publishes one `PerformedBasicAction`. A new action family inherits the rule rather than being told it, the kind travels with the fact because "a basic *skill*" narrows by it and a replacement action has no definition on the card to look one up on, and the fact is produced by the command that ends the action rather than stored. The discriminating evidence is one action kind on two cards: p.7's Hold, which every card offers and none prints an icon for, spent from Magnetic Dagger and from Lift Up - same action, opposite answers, and the only difference is the card. **The 7.9 deferral gate was split rather than widened.** It compared every registered capability against the showcase hands alone, which since 8.4 is half the cards this build deals, and Wasp's two failed it. A capability must now be on some card this build can deal; a handler implementation must additionally be reachable from a showcase card, which is the reusable core capability the 7.9 check actually names. Wasp's two pass the second half on 6.8's handler; a handler first introduced inside a hero slice still fails. **What the greyed-primary fixture lost.** Static Barrier was the 8.4 rule's Skill-shaped subject, chosen because a Skill with no effect root passes both other availability rules by construction. Composing its text discharges that case - every Wasp Skill primary now carries a composition - so the fixture states the release, and the rule's remaining subjects are 8.10's Attacks and 8.11's Defenses, carried by the sibling case that neutralises 7.8a1's rule with a minion beside the attacker. **Compatibility: schema stays v22, and the corpus is re-pinned for a registration cause and a content cause.** The decision is reject rather than migrate on the same ground as every registration cause before it: a payload written without these two capabilities was written by a build in which Magnetic Dagger's rider and Static Barrier's Skill did nothing, so restoring it here would silently re-interpret a match played under different cards; `ValidateCapabilityRegistrations` already refuses a list that is not sequence-equal, and no persisted state outside the corpus exists to strand. Measured at the byte level in a throwaway worktree at pre-change revision `e4b121d` rather than assumed, and it is exactly three things and no fourth: every one of the twelve authoritative payloads grows by exactly 68 bytes - the two registration entries, 35 and 33 with their prefixes and revisions - and changes one byte in place, the capability count `0x20 -> 0x22`; `verified-hand-setup-v22` changes 54 further single bytes, every one `'8' -> '9'`, which is Wasp's revision in eighteen definition rows and thirty-six instance references, the eleven showcase payloads being otherwise byte-identical; and the rule-event log gains eight `Turn.BasicActionPerformed` entries in total - two, four and two in three fixtures - and loses none. All three aggregate-invariance equality groups hold, which is what says the new entry is produced by the command rather than remembered. **Validation.** Complete Windows Edit Mode 1491/1491 from a baseline of 1479 and complete Play Mode 41/41 unchanged, both unfiltered, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive, and both green on their first run; cross-platform harness 1300/1300 from 1288, unblocked throughout and carrying the whole compile loop; living-documentation validator and `git diff --check` clean. **Review, four perspectives, no in-scope defect found.** Player experience: two of the five cards in the starting hand stop being greyed, and the first of them makes the mirror's Attack reachable, which is what makes p.14's Defense reaction answerable from a verified attacker rather than only from a showcase one. Rules authority: every decision is read off pp.7, 9, 12, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18 and 19 and quoted where asserted; the two places a reading could have been assumed - whether an item raises an Active effect's radius, and whether an action a basic card never printed an icon for is basic - are answered by the page rather than by convenience. Maintainability: one statement of the disk, one handler for every Active-effect creation in the build, one place p.9's supertype is decided, and the gate that would have blocked composition is now asking the question it was written to ask. Project risk: Phase 8 is open and 8.10 follows, still blocked on ledger question **W-05**. **Material limitations, recorded rather than glossed.** The pinning field's end-to-end refusal of a Place or a Swap is not demonstrated in a configured match: no card in Wasp's starting five performs either, and the only verified card that Places is Tier II. Milestone 8.7 owns those consumers and proved both; this milestone proves the declaration they read. And the `AnyTeam` arm of the new scope excludes the source itself on p.19's self-and-another reading, which no card exercises - the one that needed the scope names the other side. | `19a6def` |

## Milestone 8.8a Show upgrade choices as tray-style cards completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.8a | Rulebook p.15's Upgrade a Card presents its six choices as the tray's own complete portrait card faces, in two rows reading Blue, Green, Red. **The face is the candidate, and it is the tray's builder rather than a second card design.** `SystemTransitionOptionViewModel` carries a `TrayCardViewModel` for the card the option would put in the hand, built by `CurrentRoundCards.DeckCard` through the same `ReadFace` a tray card goes through, and drawn by `DirectorHandsUnityView.BuildStandaloneCard` - so Initiative, the printed values, the primary action with p.13's Ranged and p.14's Block qualifiers, p.9's Range or Radius badge, the exact transcribed words and the assistive colour reading all arrive without this prompt learning to decode anything. `TrayCardGroup` gained `Deck` and the band reads `IN DECK`: the tray never produces it, but borrowing `Available` would have written `AVAILABLE` on a card in nobody's hand. **The column order is the one ordering this milestone decides.** The pause groups its options by upgraded card and orders those pairs by card instance identity - Blue, Red, Green for the configured Wasp and Red, Blue, Green for the showcase hand, the same three colours in a different order per hero and in no order a player could learn - so `MatchHudTheme.ColorReadingOrder` states Blue, Green, Red once and the grid reads its columns by it. Everything else stays the projection's: which options exist, which pair each belongs to, and which candidate is drawn above the other, which is what the check that the projected order is *not* already Blue-Green-Red exists to keep honest. **The item is on the option because it is not on either card.** p.16 files the candidate p.15 sets aside under the matching dashboard slot, and that is the card this option does *not* take - so `GAINS ATK +1` under a face tells a player which of the two items each choice leaves them holding, drawn as the same compact channel token and signed value the hero rails already use. **Three user decisions taken during the Game View review, 2026-08-22, recorded in the roadmap block**: `SETS ASIDE <card>` and `RETURNS <card>` came off the six faces, because the grid already says both and both stay in each option's hover and assistive reading; the prompt must not scroll, so while the grid stands the tray region is bounded by the whole height under the status strip rather than by `CardPanelTallViewportShare`, which is the share an ordinary one-row tray may take; and the section gutter names the family beside the first row rather than halfway down two rows of cards. Nothing competes for the extra height - the ordinary tray is hidden behind a mandatory decision - and the board's framing is computed from the ordinary bound either way, so the map does not move when the prompt opens or closes; `ApplyExclusiveContext` re-lays out on the transition alone rather than every frame. **The first Game View inspection found what the extra height covered**, and it was corrected rather than accepted: the enlarged region hid the `DIRECTOR` toggle, which is the only route to Undo and no less legitimate during a mandatory decision than at any other moment. While the grid stands the toggle relocates to the band beside the Red rail under the status strip and its drawer opens downward, and `CalculateTallContextHeight` reserves that band so the toggle is clear even at the bound. Two intermediate captures were taken and rejected on the way - one with the toggle behind the region, one with it on top of the rail's own header. **Presentation-only, and the forwarding is unchanged.** The whole card is the control and forwards the invocation the continuation itself projected through `ForwardUpgradeOption`, which is also the seam a driver with no pointer uses and which refuses an option this rebuild did not draw, exactly as the presenter refuses a forged command. The selection outline is the HUD's shared non-resizing treatment on exactly one option at a time, so pointing at a candidate re-wraps no rules text and moves no row. No rule, no card state, no projection and no command changed. **A verified fixture, because the words had to be provable.** `ConfiguredMatchFixtures.CreateVerifiedMirrorPausedOnLevelUpUpgrade` reaches the same pause as the existing showcase fixture from Wasp's printed hand - Magnetic Dagger's Attack defeats the adjacent melee minion for p.13's two coins, p.15's table charges one - which is what lets a test claim a candidate's face carries the card's transcribed text rather than `NO SOURCE FACE`. **No check-paired failing observation applies:** 8.8a closes no recorded finding and pairs with no new conformance check; its evidence is the five focused fixtures and the Game View inspection. **Validation** T2 per `Testing/POLICY.md`: complete unfiltered Windows Edit Mode 1496/1496 from a baseline of 1491 and complete unfiltered Play Mode 41/41 unchanged, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive, both green on their first run; cross-platform harness 1300/1300, unchanged and expected, since everything new is `Presentation`-typed or a fixture the harness compiles without new cases. The targeted Game View inspection ran non-batch and wrote `tmp/milestone-7-8a-leveling-prompt.png` from the capture path, which now builds its six options from `WaspCardContent` instead of invented names. **No digest fixture moved and no schema cause exists** - nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire - so schema stays **v22**. `git diff --check` clean; living-documentation validator green. **Review, four perspectives, no in-scope defect found.** Player experience: a mandatory six-way decision that read as six two-name labels is now six cards a player compares the way they compare their hand, with the item each choice gains stated where the choice is made. Rules authority: nothing here decides a rule; the one thing presentation chooses is a column order the pause leaves unstated, and it is stated once and named as presentation's. Maintainability: one card builder, one colour reading order, one construction of a deck card. Project risk: Phase 8 stays open and 8.10 follows, unblocked by the 2026-08-22 W-05 amendment. **Material limitation, recorded rather than glossed:** the grid is proven at six options in three colours, which is p.15's shape from Level 1; a hero whose lowest Tier admits fewer upgradable cards draws fewer columns and one whose deck holds a single candidate draws a reserved empty cell, and neither position is reachable in the configured mirror, so both are covered structurally by the builder rather than by a fixture. | `b0be451` |

## Milestone 8.8b Refuse a Clear that can remove nothing completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.8b | A Clear with no token adjacent to the acting hero is drawn greyed, carries no command, and refuses a start atomically. **It is the fifth clause of a rule this build already had**, not a new one: milestone 6.5af refuses a Skill whose effect can apply nothing, 7.8a1 an Attack whose target query finds nobody, 8.4 a primary whose card text has no composition, 8.7 an action a declared rule forbids - each drawn greyed on `ActionOffer.IsAvailable` and refused at the start on the same predicate. `ClearActionQuery.CanRemoveAnything` is that predicate here, asked by `ClearOptionProjection` where the offer set is built and by `TurnResolutionAuthority.TryPerformClear` through the backend that owns the board, so the offer and the command cannot disagree; the refusal names `ClearCannotRemoveAnything` rather than the generic `ClearActionRejected`. **The distinction the rule turns on is exact.** p.13's "any number of tokens adjacent to you" includes zero, so a hero standing beside a token may still clear none - the empty subset is still projected and still confirmable, and `AClearBesideAToken_StaysAvailableAndStillConfirmsTheEmptySubset` is the case that holds it. What is refused is a Clear that *cannot* remove anything, never one whose chooser declines to, which is the same line 6.5af draws for a Skill. **A greyed offer had to stop carrying a command, and the gate said so.** The option is still projected - the control keeps its label and its place - but an unavailable one enumerates no `SubsetInvocations`. Without that it still projected the empty subset, exactly the `PerformClear` the rules had just started refusing, and `DeterministicMatchLoop` failed on its own invariant: "offered by the projection and refused by the rules". **What the complete-loop gate's `PerformClear` coverage had actually been.** With the refusal in place no seed of the engaged scenario that reaches an ending takes a Clear at all - across seeds 1-60 exactly one run takes one, and it reaches no ending inside the budget - so every Clear the walk had ever taken was the empty one offered beside nothing. The claim was kept rather than dropped: `TheCompleteLoop_ReachesEveryPartOfThePage7TurnAndThePage8Round` gained one bounded reachability run on seed 58, and the claim is now stronger, because the Clear it observes is offered over a real candidate. **The three reported seeds were re-measured by a stated rule** - the lowest seed reaching that ending inside half the budget - moving the Life-counter seed 11 to 22 (5 rounds, 109 commands) and the Wave seed 42 to 32 (22 rounds, 565 commands), with the Throne seed unchanged at 1; endings stayed plentiful across seeds 1-40, which is the evidence this was a re-indexing rather than a loss. `UndoCheckpointReconstructionTests` and `CompleteMatchRuntimeCaptureTests` pin the Wave seed deliberately and moved with it. **Recorded as `DEV-007`**, because p.13 does permit performing a Clear that removes nothing; p.12's Hold makes refusing costless, no card in this build reads "performs a Clear" as a trigger, and 8.9's `PerformedBasicAction` already carries the kind that would let one - which is why the deviation names that as its review trigger rather than assuming the case away. **Pre-fix observation, recorded on both governed checks.** `TurnResolutionRejectionReason.ClearCannotRemoveAnything` and `ClearOfferViewModel.IsAvailable` were added inert first, so the two behavioural cases compiled against the unfixed tree and were observed failing there - `AClearWithNoAdjacentToken_IsOfferedGreyedAndCarriesNoCommand` on "Expected: False / But was: True" for the offer's availability, and `AStartNamingAClearThatCanRemoveNothing_IsRefusedAndChangesNothing` on the same for the start's acceptance - with the guard case passing at both revisions, which is what says the fix did not over-reach. Filtered pre-fix run: 20 passed, 2 failed, kept at `tmp/88b-editmode-prefix-results.xml`. **Validation** T2 per `Testing/POLICY.md`: complete unfiltered Windows Edit Mode 1501/1501 from a baseline of 1496 and complete unfiltered Play Mode 41/41 unchanged, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1301/1301 from 1300, the +1 being the one Unity-independent case (`ClearPresentationTests` is Presentation-typed and excluded). Edit Mode took two runs, both faults test-side and neither in production behaviour: an `Is.SameAs` on the projection object where a refused command still returns a fresh instance, and an opacity assertion that read an unset style as zero. **No digest fixture moved and no schema cause exists** - availability is derived and the refusal is a command result, so nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire, which the harness proves by running both digest fixtures green. Living-documentation validator and `git diff --check` clean. **Review, four perspectives, no in-scope defect found.** Player experience: the control that read as an ordinary choice while the hero stood beside nothing now reads as what it is, and Hold is still there to spend the card. Rules authority: the one thing refused is something the page permits, so it is recorded as a deviation with a review trigger rather than presented as the rule. Maintainability: one query answers both askers, and the fifth clause is a clause rather than a fifth place the offer set is post-processed. Project risk: Phase 8 stays open and 8.10 follows. **Material limitation, recorded rather than glossed:** a Clear that actually removes a token is rare enough in the generated walk that one seed in sixty takes one, so the gate's coverage of it rests on that single recorded seed; the behavioural coverage of removal itself is `ClearActionTests` and `ClearPresentationTests`, which take pieces off the board through the full command path. | `9a67774` |

## Milestone 8.10 Complete the Wasp Attack cards completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.10 | All five of Wasp's printed Attacks resolve their complete text, and the boundary that had blocked them is gone: **card text applied inside an Attack can stop on a question**. **It landed at two of rulebook p.14's three moments, and the third is a stated rule rather than an omission.** What the page leaves after step 4 and after step 7 is a function of the attack's own identity - step 5's Defense total with the comparison step 6 opens, or the outcome and the end of the action - so `AttackDefenseCardActionStrategyBackend.StepsAfterCardText` produces it, and it is the *same* generator that emits those steps when nothing pauses at all, which is what makes the paused and unpaused orders unable to drift apart. What follows step 1 is not such a function: on the arm where p.13 simply defeats a targeted minion it contains the facts of a Defeat the resolver has already computed and applied, and no identity reproduces those. `EffectDefinitionValidator.ValidateCardText` therefore still refuses a composition that could ask there, now taking the moment as an argument so the refusal names step 1 instead of the whole Attack. Every verified face this milestone and 8.11 need prints "After the attack:" or Defense-action text, so nothing verified is excluded by the boundary. **The continuation persists identity and never steps.** `CardActionContinuationKind.AttackCardText` carries the paused frame - the same section a paused Skill writes, resumed through the same `EffectResolutionSession.Answer` - beside the moment the page stopped at and the attack whose remainder is owed. `CaptureRestoreState` asks about it *before* the attack, because at step 4 the attack's own resolution is still pending underneath the paused text and asking the other way round would keep the attack and lose the frame holding the page open. **Shock and Electrocute share one composition, and that is content rather than economy**: the two print the same sentence and differ only in the printed Radius icon, which the selection reads off the card. The new `synthetic-radius-hero-target` is p.9's disk without its innermost ring, on the side the card names, and it is implemented by the *existing* `PrintedIconTargetHandler` under a declared subject mode - so p.9's geometry, p.18's immunity and p.16's item composition are asked in one place for all four printed-icon declarations, and milestone 7.9's deferral gate stays green on composition rather than on an exclusion entry. The selection deliberately declares no consuming node: narrowing the offer to heroes holding a card would make the printed `if able` unreachable text, which is the opposite of what the face says. **Electroblast escalates on ledger question 13's reading, and asks nobody anything extra.** `DiscardChosenCardV2` reports whether the chosen hero had a card rather than declining in silence - revision 1 stays live for the two `if able` printings, because a declined step publishes nothing by design - and one `Conditional` arm runs p.13's list where they did not. The Defeat is a registered capability that **declares and performs nothing**, which is milestone 6.9's mid-turn Lane-Push shape: p.13's list prices a reward by Level, spends Life counters, returns markers, resolves an unresolved card and cancels Active effects, all of it authority state no capability holds and all of it already written once in the Attack resolver, so `RequestedHeroDefeatQuery` reads the request off the completed frame and `ResolveEffectInvokedHeroDefeat` runs the page. It is consumed only on a *completed* step, the same rule the Lane-Push cause is read under and for the same reason: a frame carries its outcomes cumulatively, so a request read per step would run the page twice about one hero. **Three of that resolver's event factories had been reading the defeated hero off the Attack's own target** and threw the first time a rider defeated somebody else; they now take it explicitly, with the pairing guard kept and pointed at the right hero. **Thunder Boomerang's three printed clauses became three declarations.** `synthetic-attack-took-a-hero` reads the enclosing Attack's target from the resolution context - the way `EnclosingActionKind` arrives, because the condition is about the action the text is printed inside rather than about anything the text did - `May` is p.10's confirm-or-decline, and `RepeatTargetBinding.DifferentTarget` at maximum 1 excludes the unit the first instance took. The exclusion rides `PendingAttackResolution.ExcludedTarget` and crosses the wire, because the legal target set is re-derived every time it is asked and a filter applied only at the offer would not survive restore; the binding rides `RepeatRequest`, because the dispatcher may not read effect nodes and a conformance rule fails the build when it does. `StartRepeatedAttack` bypasses offer availability deliberately - by the time a repeat is owed the card has been played, which is precisely why p.10 has a repeat - and everything after that point is the ordinary Attack: its own target choice, its own Defense response, its own p.13 consequences and p.14 order. **Charged Boomerang is the one composed Wasp Attack with no rider at all**: its whole printed text is the targeting sentence, declared as the third dimension of `PrintedAttackTargetPolicy`, so it carries no effect root and owes nothing, and the policy refuses the one combination that could never target anything - p.19 counts an adjacent space as a straight line, so excluding the line while reaching only adjacent units is a card that can never act. **Milestone 8.4's honesty rule is left with no turn-action subject in Wasp's set**, exactly as 8.9's composing Static Barrier removed the Skill one; its remaining verified subjects are 8.11's three Defense primaries, which are performed in reaction and which no start site asks the question of yet, so the greyed case here became the release and **8.11 owes that rule its refusal case** - recorded in the roadmap block and in the fixture's own doc comment rather than left to be discovered. **Pre-fix observation, recorded on three statements at revision `381dc5b`**: that all five Attacks owe their composition, carry no card text and narrow no targeting; that Shock's Attack is drawn greyed and refused, so neither question its rider asks can be reached; and that card text which could pause is refused for every one of p.14's moments. All three passed at `381dc5b` and all three fail against this revision - the third no longer even compiles, because `ValidateCardText` now demands the moment, which is the transition itself. **Validation** T3 per `Testing/POLICY.md`, because the milestone moves the persisted schema: complete unfiltered Windows Edit Mode 1511/1511 from a baseline of 1501 and complete unfiltered Play Mode 41/41 unchanged in both total and composition, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1311/1311 from 1301, the two tiers agreeing on the delta exactly because all ten new cases are Unity-independent. Edit Mode took two runs and **the first failed to compile rather than to pass**: `Does.Not.Contain` over a typed collection binds to the string overload in Unity's NUnit 3.5 and to the collection overload in the harness's newer NUnit, which is `RISK-VALIDATION-001` precisely; `Has.No.Member` is what both agree on, and the scratch Presentation compile probe confirmed both tiers before the relaunch because it references Unity's own `nunit.framework.dll`. **Schema v22 to v23, with the full ceremony.** Both sections are ones a v22 payload cannot describe - the card-text continuation and the repeated instance's excluded target - and v22 is rejected rather than migrated on the standing ground: the only reading available for either absence is "nothing is paused" and "this instance may take anyone", both true of every payload a v22 build could write and both false of the first payload this milestone writes. Twelve corpus fixtures renamed to `-v23` and re-pinned, a thirteenth added - `paused-card-text-v23`, because the continuation is written only while p.14 is stopped and a section no fixture holds is protected vacuously - the nine aggregate digests re-pinned with all three equality groups intact, the two bare version assertions moved, and all six documents `Tools/PinnedConstants.txt` names. **The byte delta was measured in a throwaway worktree at `381dc5b` rather than assumed**, and it is exactly two things: every one of the twelve renamed payloads grows by exactly 126 bytes and by nothing else, which is four new capability registration entries at 33, 26, 33 and 34 bytes with their prefixes and revisions, and `verified-hand-setup` grows by 180, those same 126 plus one character in each of the 54 places Wasp's content revision appears. **The two new sections add nothing to any of the twelve**, which is the measurement saying in bytes why the thirteenth fixture had to exist. Living-documentation validator green at 127/127 self-tests, 58 leaves, 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean. **Review, four perspectives, no in-scope defect found.** Player experience: five cards that were drawn greyed and refused are now playable, and the two questions Wasp's riders ask are asked of the people p.10 assigns them to - the actor picks the hero, the hero picks the card. Rules authority: the one interpretive call is ledger question 13's, already answered from source at 8.9 and implemented here as a branch rather than a question; the one new boundary, p.14 step 1, is stated with its reason rather than left as a silent limit. Maintainability: one generator for the page's remainder, one place asking p.9's disk for both selection subjects, one copy of p.13's Defeat list, and three new declarations on two implementations. Project risk: Phase 8 stays open and 8.11 follows, carrying the refusal case this milestone's release leaves it. **Material limitations, recorded rather than glossed:** the repeated Attack is proven on a board where the second instance has exactly one remaining legal target, so the case that a repeat with *no* remaining target simply does not happen is covered by the backend's own refusal path rather than by a fixture; and card text pausing at p.14's step 4 is implemented and reachable but has no verified card exercising it until 8.11's Block riders, so its coverage today is the generator it shares with step 7 rather than a configured case of its own. | `7b317f4` |

## Milestone 8.11 Complete the Wasp Defense cards completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.11 | Stop Projectiles, Deflect Projectiles and Reflect Projectiles resolve their complete printed text as rulebook p.14 Block cards, and the reserved seam they land on became a real field. **`DefenseBlockResult` is a fact the Attack resolution carries, not a Defense number.** Milestone 5.8 reserved it; this milestone gives it `ConditionUnfulfilled` beside `Fulfilled` and `None`, evaluates it at p.14's step 4 from the Attack's own typed subtype facts, and writes it on the resolution state - which is why the schema moves 23 -> 24. A Block never produces a Defense amount at all: `DefenseValueQuery.Calculate` is not called on the Block arm, so step 5's comparison is skipped rather than fed, and no sentinel can be mistaken for a printed value. Fulfilled is a successful defense whatever the Attack total; unfulfilled defeats the defender through the same staged p.13 chain 8.10 registered as a capability, not through a second copy of the page. **Both riders compose the defender-anchored Range channel from the card's own printed icon**, one enemy hero in range discarding a card if able, chosen by the defender per p.10 - and "another enemy hero" on Deflect is a parameter of the Range channel alone, `excludeTheAttacker`, resolved from `EnclosingAttackFacts.ActorId` because the attacker is a fact about the action being answered rather than about anything the text did. Declaring it per channel is what keeps the three Radius selections' declarations byte-identical, which the corpus measurement confirms rather than assumes. **The Block keeps its `PrintedPrimary` origin**, per milestone 8.3's rule that a printed primary is recorded as one: what widened is the offer window and the invocation context, and the two sites that had encoded "a Defense is a Reaction" as an origin check - `PendingDefenseResponseChoice` and the completed-defense match - were taught the printed Block rather than relaxed. **Milestone 8.4's honesty rule got the refusal case 8.10 recorded as owed**, on the Defense path, through a new public `DefenseOfferQuery` that asks both p.11's restriction and the composition question before offering a card; its subject is synthetic on purpose, because after this milestone no verified Defense owes a composition and a case built on Wasp's own cards would assert over nothing. Seven configured cases in `ConfiguredDefenseCardTests`; the full harness stands at **1318 tests, all passing**, up from 1311. **The failing-then-passing evidence was taken by knockout rather than by revert**, since the types and the tests landed together: removing the Reaction half of the window fails all four Block-behaviour cases and the composition case; forcing the Block verdict to `None` fails the same four; deleting the attacker exclusion fails Deflect alone with `Expected: not equal to blue-test-hero`. That exercise found a real defect in a test rather than in the code - `ABlock_IsNeverOfferedAsATurnActionAndNeverFromAPlayedCard` claimed the window was what held a played Block out, and passed with the window removed, because the Hand -> DiscardPile transition the Block gains this milestone excludes it independently. The case now pins the outcome and names both rules instead of claiming one. **The schema move carries the full ceremony.** v23 is rejected rather than migrated on this corpus's standing ground: the only reading available for the field's absence is "no Block condition was ever evaluated", true of every payload a v23 build could write and false of the first payload this milestone writes. All thirteen fixtures were renamed and a fourteenth added, `paused-block-rider-v24`, which earned its place immediately - it caught a Conditional root declaring an output its predicate never published, on this milestone's rider and latently on 8.10's `thunder-repeat-root`, where no other fixture looked. The delta was measured in a throwaway worktree at pre-change revision `6ed3144f` rather than assumed: every renamed payload grows by exactly 63 bytes, the two new capability registrations `synthetic-attack-is-ranged` and `synthetic-range-hero-target` at 31 and 32 bytes with their length prefixes and revisions, plus the capability count `0x26 -> 0x28` in place; `paused-card-text-v24` grows by 67, the extra four being the new field itself, since it is the only renamed fixture holding an Attack resolution. Wasp's content revision `wasp-8-10-rev-1` -> `wasp-8-11-rev-1` is the same width and moves nothing. **A gap in 8.10's own ceremony was closed here**: 8.10 re-pinned all nine aggregate-invariance digests without recording why, which is the one thing that fixture's own note requires of a re-pinning commit. Its delta was measured after the fact in a worktree at `19a6def8` - schema 22 -> 23 plus four capability registrations at 126 bytes on every carried payload, and 180 on the verified-hand one, the extra 54 being `wasp-8-9-rev-1` -> `wasp-8-10-rev-1` one character longer in each of its 54 occurrences - and written into the fixture as the thirty-eighth re-pin beside this milestone's thirty-ninth. All three equality groups held across both. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** the Block verdict has no continuous-source or trigger interaction yet, because neither exists until 8.12; `ConditionUnfulfilled` is exercised only by Stop Projectiles, the one Block whose condition can fail on a board this fixture set can build; and Deflect's exclusion is proved on a two-hero board, where excluding the attacker leaves nobody, so the case asserts that the defender is never asked rather than that a third hero is offered - the attacker's own text still resolves at p.14 step 7 regardless of the Block's outcome, which is why that case asserts the absence of a defender question and not the absence of a pending choice. | `82c2c09` |

## Milestone 8.11a Official map underlay and transparent interaction overlay completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.11a | The configured match draws the official `GuardsII_Small_Board` face beneath the reviewed 292-space map, and - by a user-approved scope amendment - every choice with a board surface is answered on the board. **The artwork is private and stays private.** `Tools/Prepare-OfficialBoardArtwork.ps1` reads the bundled Tabletopia manifest, takes the 1588x1588 sprite that element declares out of the `Image_17_Atlas` it names, and writes exactly one ignored file under `Assets/Resources/Private/`; the corpus and the atlas are read-only to it, and a clean checkout has no artwork and plays through the generated board unchanged. The crop is read from the manifest rather than assumed, and the script refuses to write if the atlas it finds does not match the declared dimensions - which is how the first attempt was caught, reading the sprite rectangle bottom-up and putting a neighbouring sprite along the top edge of the crop. **The registration was settled against the artwork, not against the magnetic map.** The manifest's 332 magnetic centres fit a single hexagonal lattice exactly, 332 distinct indices at worst 10.4% of the 2.4074-unit spacing, and they are stated in the sprite's own frame - confirmed by drawing all 332 onto the extracted face and finding every one on a painted hex. What they could not settle is orientation: the twelve lattice symmetries score 272 to 283 of 292, so the best-scoring one is not meaningfully better than the others and taking it would have been taking noise. It was settled by drawing the map's seven zones over the face instead, where exactly one orientation puts every zone boundary on a painted boundary - the Red Throne on the drill, the Blue Throne on the crystal, both jungles on the forests, the terrain ring on the rock - and **the highest-scoring lattice match is not that orientation**. The fitted transform is a similarity and nothing looser, measured over the 275 spaces that have a centre: **mean deviation 0.031 and worst 0.076 board units, which is 0.054 world units, 5.4% of a space's outer radius**, against a declared tolerance of a tenth of a space. Shear and mirroring are excluded by test rather than by hope, because a landmark table can see neither. **The framing camera stands on the board's own axes, by the user's decision**: the hex lattice sits about fifteen degrees inside the square, so the square and the lane cannot both be square to the screen, and the artwork wins - the square reads upright as it does on the table, and the lane runs corner to corner because on this board it does. `BoardChoiceCue` takes its up from the same statement so every board-facing cue turns with the framing; `StandardMinionView` had that rotation written out by hand and was the one thing that stayed crooked until it read the shared construction. **Nothing was removed to make room for the picture.** Tile fills become one fully transparent material, debug edges and spawn rings switch their renderers off, and every view stays configured - so selection, highlighting and the presentation tests read exactly what they read before, and removing the artwork restores all of it on the next rebuild. **A hex you choose is lit; a piece you choose is marked.** Ranges, correction destinations, respawn spaces and empty effect-choice candidates keep the outlined region, because the answer is the space and only lighting shows a region as a region; Attack targets and effect candidates naming a unit or a token wear the Minion Battle's own marker, because the answer is the thing standing there. That split is also the only thing that reads over this board - a tint must contrast with pale sand, mid brown, jungle and near-black rock at once and no colour does, while the marker carries its own dark backing. Highlights are drawn see-through with an opaque rim: the rim carries identity and shape and keeps the gold that has meant "selectable" since milestone 1.5, and the fill only makes the space look lit. Two earlier palettes were tried and rejected in play - gold as a blended fill read as slightly yellower sand, and violet read clearly but looked alien because nothing in the artwork is that colour. **Board targeting.** Effect-choice candidates that name a space, Hero Respawn destinations and Minion Battle candidates are answered by clicking the board; the prompt keeps only what has no board surface - declining to respawn, Fast Travel selection, Upgrade a Card, and the Battle's confirm. Marking every candidate rather than only the hovered one required the board to read the authority's `LegalSelectUnits` rather than its `Candidates`, because rulebook p.8 removes Heavy minions last and the raw list offered a Heavy the rules refuse. **A Block was unreachable, and milestone 8.11 shipped it that way.** The defence preview calculated a Defense value for every offer, so a Block previewed as a card worth zero and predicted the defender losing an attack it in fact stops; the prompt then looked for the offer's printed Defense icon, found none, and threw where it was drawn - the authority asked, the screen asked nothing, and the match stopped. p.14's condition now lives in one `DefenseBlockQuery` that both the resolver and the preview ask, because a preview that disagreed with the resolution would be worse than none; a Block previews with no total, no contributions, its real verdict, and the outcome that verdict produces, and the prompt names it by what it does. One projection guard was relaxed by exactly one case: a Defense-card preview may have no Total, but only when it states a Block verdict. **The gap was not the Block.** Milestone 8.11's cases all drove the authority directly and none ever built the prompt a defender is shown, so `PromptReachabilityConformanceTests` now checks the authority's offers against the surfaces a player answers them on - every legal Defense produces exactly one distinct control and building the prompt must not throw; every effect-choice candidate is answerable on the board or in the prompt; and the Battle marks exactly the legal selections and nothing the rules withhold. It asserts reachability rather than appearance, because what a control says is a judgement and whether an offered answer can be given at all is not. Complete Edit Mode **1533 passed / 0 failed**, complete Play Mode **41 passed / 0 failed**, cross-platform harness **1319 passed**; no schema move, and no digest fixture moved, because nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** an effect-choice candidate that names a hero by identity rather than by coordinate still answers through a prompt control, because the board is addressed by space and that candidate names none - nothing is unreachable, and the conformance case proves it, but "board only" is not yet literally true; milestone 8.11c owns closing it. The artwork overflows the framed band at its corners by design, since the framing contains the authoritative map rather than the picture. And `Unlit/Texture` had to be declared in Graphics settings' always-included shaders, at a file id measured from the Editor rather than guessed, after two attempts to render the face with the Standard shader failed - albedo and emission both carrying the texture doubled it, and blacking the albedo left the specular and reflection response to this scene's flat blue-grey ambient, which bleached it even with both paths switched off. | `b89f7b4` |

## Milestone 8.11b Replace Wasp pins and abbreviated card indicators with verified card-table visuals completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |

| 8.11b | Three surfaces stopped abbreviating the thing they stand for. **The hero is the official token, and the team is stated twice.** `Tools/Prepare-WaspHeroTokens.ps1` reads the bundled Tabletopia manifest, takes the two 302x302 faces element `8597310` (`Wasp`, `VerticalTokens`) declares in the `Image_1_Atlas` it names, and writes exactly two ignored files under `Assets/Resources/Private/Heroes/`; the corpus and the atlas are read-only to it, and a clean checkout has neither and keeps the generated prism. **Which face is which team is measured, not taken from their order**: the script samples each face's ring all the way round at 96% of the radius and averages it, so one dark passage of the portrait cannot decide it, then applies the reviewed milestone 4.4 mapping - the face at (0,2539) averages R127 G189 B215 and is Crystal/light-blue to Blue, the face at (304,2539) averages R239 G130 B27 and is Steam/orange to Red - and refuses to write unless exactly one of each is found. The alpha is the inscribed disc, which was measured rather than assumed: the atlas background runs from each corner to within a pixel of that circle and the four edge midpoints are already ring, and the script checks both ends of that claim before writing. At runtime the face is *selected* by projected content identity and authoritative team, exactly as `StandardMinionArtCatalog` selects a minion's, and nothing asks a pixel anything. **The source set rings its faces orange and light blue, which are its team colours and not this project's**, so a player should not have to know that orange means Red: the piece carries a generated rim in this project's own board Red or Blue, read from `TeamSide`, and `HeroTokenArtwork.TeamRim` is now the single statement of those two colours that the prism reads as well - the board's Red is one Red whichever piece is standing on it. The swap is a mesh and two materials on the view's own `MeshFilter`/`MeshRenderer`, two submeshes on one renderer rather than a second object, so the `HeroId` everything binds to, the choice cue, movement, Defeat and respawn visibility, Undo, restart, restore, replay and rebuild are untouched by it. The disc is a disc rather than a quad because `Unlit/Texture` is opaque and a quad would render the cleared corners black; no new shader was needed, so no Graphics-settings entry moved. `PrimitiveHeroPointerSelector` builds its capsule from the new `PrimitiveHeroView.SelectionRadius`, which answers `TokenOuterRadius` 0.81 with a token and `DefaultBaseRadius` 0.40 without - milestone 8.11a's own rule that the pointer target grows with the piece so picking stays what a player sees. **A Defense offer is the card, not a summary of it.** Every legal offer is drawn through `DirectorHandsUnityView.BuildStandaloneCard`, the same builder the tray and p.15's upgrade grid use, at one fixed `DefenseCardFootprint` so choosing one cannot re-wrap or resize another; the whole face is the control and forwards the offer's own projected invocation through `ForwardDefenseCard`, which is the path its own `Button` calls, and an offer this rebuild did not draw forwards nothing. Nothing about the decision moved onto the card: the authority-computed Attack/Defense/result preview is still raised from the offer's own projected breakdown, visible order is the projected response order and therefore the keyboard order, and `DECLINE DEFENSE` is about no card, carries none, and stays the compact content-sized control milestone 5.1c's clipping guarantee is about. **The rail draws cards instead of numbers.** The four resolved-turn slots lost the ordinals `1` to `4` and the discard row lost its count and its one-pip-per-colour strip; each is now one compact card-shaped icon per card, in that card's own printed colour, carrying no text at all. Sequence is the row's fixed left-to-right positions and the count is how many icons there are. An unoccupied slot stays an empty outline that reveals nothing and answers no pointer. Resting on any occupied icon opens the same single `gd-turn-slot-preview` the slots and the current-card marker already shared, so two faces can never stand at once and moving between icons replaces the one that is up; each icon's assistive label names the exact card and its lifecycle location. **The discard pile needed a projection change, not a drawing one**: `HeroOverviewViewModel` carried a count and a bare colour list, and one icon per card needs the instances - `CurrentRoundCards.DiscardedCards` now supplies them, ordered by card instance identity, which is exactly what the tray already orders its own discarded group by, so one card sits in the same place on both surfaces and the order survives restore and replay rather than being read off discard events the presenter watched. Complete Edit Mode **1541 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, complete Play Mode **42 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, cross-platform harness **1319 passed** and unmoved, which is the signature of an entirely `Presentation` milestone - the harness excludes all three touched fixtures by name. **No schema move and no digest fixture moved**, because nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire. **The failing-then-passing evidence was taken by knockout in five runs**, since the types and the tests landed together: restoring the slot ordinal and restoring the discard count each fail the icon case on their own assertion, reverting `BuildDefense` to text controls fails all three Defense cases, making the face address team-blind fails the addressing case with `Expected: "Private/Heroes/wasp-blue" But was: "Private/Heroes/wasp-red"`, and pinning the collider to the prism radius fails the Play Mode token case with `Expected: 0.810000002f But was: 0.400000006f`. **The second knockout found a defect in a test rather than in the code**: the discard-count assertion and the slot-ordinal loop both trip on a pile holding one to four cards, so the count check could pass or fail for the wrong reason - it was rewritten against the discard row's own label set and moved ahead of the ordinal loop, where it stands alone. One targeted non-batch Game View inspection at 2560x1440 covered both team-coloured Wasp tokens on the official board, three numeric Defense faces beside Stop Projectiles' Block face, `DECLINE DEFENSE`, and both rails' number-free rows; **it cost two passes on layout the batch tier cannot see** - content-sized offers came out four different heights at four different baselines, and stretching the frame alone left the face top-aligned inside it. That inspection also corrected the capture itself: the existing Play Mode Defense case took the first four hand cards, which dropped the only Block in Wasp's starting hand, and it now chooses its four so a Block is among them and names each offer from the card rather than from an invented string. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** the rim is a flat generated annulus rather than a miniature base, and milestone 8.11d's target rings will sit immediately outside it - the two must be checked to read as two things and not one band; the mini-card icon deliberately carries no name or value, because at 24 pixels a name band is an unreadable smudge and a smudge that looks like text is worse than none; the discard row wraps but is not scrolled, which is sufficient only because p.8's retrieval empties the pile every round; and only Wasp has prepared faces, so `HeroTokenArtwork`'s addressing is proved for a second hero by its resource path rather than by a second image. | `6ca6b41` |

## Milestone 8.11c Answer identity-named effect-choice candidates on the board completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.11c | Milestone 8.11a put every candidate that names a *space* on the board and recorded, as a stated limitation, that one shape was left behind: a candidate naming a hero by identity still answered through a prompt control, because the board is addressed by space and such a candidate names none. This closes it. **The fix is one reading, in the place the roadmap named as its preferred home.** `EffectChoiceCandidatePreview` now carries the candidate's own `EffectValue` beside the space, and `EffectChoicePreviewQuery` resolves that space rather than restating it: a coordinate is taken as given and read for whatever stands on it, while a `HeroId` or a `MinionId` is put through `IBoardDisplacementAuthority.TryGetObjectCoordinate`, which is authoritative occupancy and nothing else. Both shapes therefore arrive at the same (value, space, occupant) triple, and every consumer downstream addresses a candidate by its value and the board by its space. Nothing reads a card, a hero name, or a node name, so the next definition that publishes a hero is answered by this code rather than by its own. **What that made true, in four places that had each hard-coded `EffectValueKind.Coordinate`.** `CreateEffectChoices` keys its preview lookup by candidate value, so a hero-named candidate becomes an `EffectChoiceTargetViewModel` named after that hero - and therefore leaves `EffectChoices`, which is the list the prompt draws, so its control is withdrawn without the view ever inspecting a candidate's shape. `SelectableEffectChoiceSpaces` matches invocations to previews by value, so the board lights and marks those spaces. `SubmitEffectChoiceAtSpace` goes the other way - the click names a space, the preview says which candidate value stands there, and the invocation is found by that value - which means milestone 8.11a's existing hero, minion and empty-space click routes all reach the new shape with no new path. And milestone 6.5b's supply arm now asks the board rather than the value's kind: a minion named by identity is a Spawn candidate only when occupancy has it standing nowhere, which is the one value shape the board still has nothing to offer. **The totality claim is a failure, not a comment.** `TryGetObjectAt` answers with at most one object per space, so two candidates resolving to one space would be two answers to one click; the query throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the space rather than collapsing them into a single mark or quietly dropping one back to a control. A candidate naming an object that is on no space is simply not previewed and keeps its control - the honest outcome rather than a fault, unreachable for the live content, and guarded by the conformance case below, which asks occupancy the same question independently. **Reflect Projectiles' rider is the live subject** and it is not alone: `synthetic-range-hero-target` (Deflect and Reflect Projectiles) and `synthetic-radius-hero-target` (Shock, Electrocute, Electroblast) both publish the hero rather than the space, because what consumes the choice is a p.10 discard and a space cannot be asked for a card. **The failing-then-passing observation is the roadmap's own**: milestone 8.11a's `PromptReachabilityConformanceTests` case tightened from "answerable on the board or in the prompt" to "answered on the board, with no prompt control" for every candidate that names a board object, and it was observed failing at the pre-fix revision with `Expected: collection containing (-2, -5) But was: <empty>` - the space the attacker was standing on, on neither board list - then passing after. It decides which candidates name an object by reading `projection.Heroes`, `projection.Minions` and `projection.PlacedTokens` directly rather than by consulting the preview, so it cannot agree with whatever the preview happens to cover. Four further cases: two in `EffectChoicePreviewTests` for the hero preview landing on projected occupancy with no resulting space - a discard relocates nobody - and for the two-on-one-space refusal naming its space; two in `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` for the hero becoming a named target with no anonymous select left in the prompt, and for `SubmitHero` answering the choice with the candidate that names the clicked hero. Complete Edit Mode **1545 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, complete Play Mode **42 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive** and unmoved, cross-platform harness **1319 to 1321**, the two tiers disagreeing by two because `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` is `Presentation`-typed and excluded while `EffectChoicePreviewTests` is not. **No schema move and no digest fixture moved**: the preview is derived per projection and reaches no payload, which both digest fixtures prove by running green inside the complete suites. The Undo, restart, restore, replay and rebuild clause is carried by that same property plus the existing `ThePreviewIsRebuiltFromCurrentStateRatherThanRemembered` case and the directly affected regressions in both complete suites - nothing here remembers a space, so there is no lifetime to survive. **A documentation ownership review came due with it.** `Project/CONFIGURED_PRESENTATION.md` sat one word under its 85% line after 8.11b, and its milestone 6.5c paragraph had been left stale by 8.11a - it still said a candidate naming a board object "carries one control labelled after that object", which 8.11a removed. That paragraph is rewritten to what is now true and 8.11c is stated after it; the milestone 5.1b/5.1c Defense-label paragraph, which `MATCH_HUD.md` has owned since 7.8a and fully covered since 8.11b, is reduced to the boundary it still owns plus a pointer, and the 2048-by-1152 and 778-by-300 viewport evidence it carried moved into that owner rather than being dropped. The shard ends under its warning line without losing a statement. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** no `EffectValue` arm carries a `TokenInstanceId`, so a token candidate is still a coordinate candidate and reaches the board through the occupant reading it always did - the roadmap's mention of one is satisfied by that path rather than by a new arm; a `CardInstance`-valued candidate is deliberately never resolved, because p.10's discard chooses among cards in a hand and a hand is not a space; and the minion-by-identity path is proved by the preview and the supply-arm branch rather than by live content, because no verified card publishes a standing minion yet. | `fc0070a` |

## Milestone 8.16 Complete the deterministic Wasp mirror integration gate completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.16 | The verified Wasp mirror plays itself from rulebook p.5's setup to each of the three endings p.8 and p.13 name. `WaspMirrorIntegrationGateTests` points milestone 7.9's `DeterministicMatchLoop` at `WaspCardContent` instead of the mechanics-showcase hands: nothing is scripted, the loop answers whichever decision the authoritative projection is actually waiting on, and four reported seeds - measured under one written rule, the lowest reaching that ending inside half the budget having crossed ten round boundaries - end by the last Life counter, by the final Wave flip, by a Throne capture at an end-of-round push, and by a Throne capture in the middle of a turn. Determinism, the replay oracle, halfway restore-and-continue, retained-prefix Undo across the ending, restart, the rule-event log and `DEV-005`'s Tie Breaker override are each proved on that mirror rather than on a fixture, a four-seed bounded soak plays many rounds of the ordinary composition and round-trips every final state through both wire forms, and every card these matches committed is checked to be one of Wasp's eighteen dealt `VerifiedOfficial` faces with all five of p.5's starting cards played and the boxed Handicap card absent. **The gate found two production defects and both are fixed here.** p.8's mid-turn Lane Push is consumed inside `TurnResolutionAuthority.AdvanceInitiative`, so a push that flipped the final Wave or captured the Throne there ended the match while `RoundResolutionAuthority` was asking only whether p.13's Life counters had run out: the phase never recorded the ending, and the next projection threw `UnrecordedGameEnd`, refusing an offered command as an `EngineFault` - two of sixty engaged seeds could not be played to their end at all. The turn-command boundary now asks which ending the authoritative state holds, through `MatchOutcomeQuery` with the phase answered as not-recorded, and admits only that one fault so a conflicting-paths payload stays an engine defect. Behind it, the payload of such a match could not be restored: the turn it stops is started, unended, actorless and holds no request, a fourth legitimate shape the reader refused as incomplete, so `ReadTurn` now passes down the `GameEnded` phase it had already read. Both are pinned by deterministic fixtures rather than by a seed - `MatchVictoryPathTests.AMidTurnLanePushThatFlipsTheFinalWave_EndsTheMatchInsideTheTurnItHappenedIn` and `AMatchEndedByAMidTurnLanePush_RestoresToTheSameOutcome`, each observed failing at the pre-fix revision with the exact `UnrecordedGameEnd` and `MalformedPayload` rejections and passing after. The ending they assert is distinguished from the end-of-round arm structurally rather than by fixture claim: a mid-turn push names no end-of-round stage. T3 closed at the final revision with both complete suites green on their first unfiltered run: Edit Mode 1599/1599, Play Mode 66/66, cross-platform harness 1365/1365, zero failed, skipped or inconclusive. All fourteen new cases are Unity-independent and both tiers moved by exactly fourteen. Schema stays v24 with no digest moved and no Presentation, scene, asset or assembly change; the ending is derived from state already persisted and the reader admits a shape it previously refused rather than writing a new field. ## Milestone 8.16a Independently review Wasp-driven card-engine extensibility completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.16a | Fresh-task analysis-only independent review of Wasp-driven card-engine extensibility at working tree `0f371fc8`, whose code is byte-identical to 8.16's gate revision `e7e470e5` (the one-line delta is this archive's own SHA row). **Verdict: the generic card/effect architecture is ready for five more heroes, and Wasp revealed that its remaining costs sit in the evidence and identity machinery around the engine rather than in the engine itself.** Nineteen printed faces reached production with no card-name branch, no hero-name branch, no runtime printed-text parse, and no untyped payload anywhere in `Core`, `Application`, or `Presentation`; the delta register's central claim - "no custom handler anywhere in Wasp's set" - was verified true in code, with both Active-effect cards riding one generic `ActiveEffectCreationHandler` and all six printed-icon selections riding one `PrintedIconTargetHandler`. **Evidence hygiene:** the 8.16 gate XMLs in `tmp/` were opened and their run attributes reconciled against the commit body (Edit 1599/1599 at 114 s, Play 66/66 at 8 s, one sitting, timestamps coherent); a recorded diagnostic run of the cross-platform harness at this revision passed 1365/1365 in 64 s; schema pin v24 and the fourteen `-v24` corpus fixtures agree with the `RISK-COMPAT-001` row. **The capability trace covered every implemented matrix row** - all nineteen printed-value rows match `WaspCardContent.Profiles()` field for field, all transcriptions match verbatim, and every capability resolves to a named typed contract, a generic handler, its serialized continuation, its ordered events, and focused evidence; nothing is untraceable. **The six leakage scans returned recorded negatives** - zero card or hero identity literals outside content, transcriptions mechanically inert under an IL conformance scan and never written to a payload, `EffectValueKind` a closed eight-member union, the five-times-repeated select-then-discard rider shape assessed and deliberately NOT abstracted (four of five axes vary; the trigger to revisit is the second hero printing the same shape), and Crackling Dagger's deferred narrowing already proved by a synthetic twin so the deferral costs no coverage. **Measured results that drove the findings** (throwaway probe, run twice, byte-identical captured-byte figures, then deleted): the complete-match runtime capture still measures the retired ten-card showcase deck while 8.16's verified twin `CreateEngagedVerifiedMirrorSession` sits unused, and its 4 KiB captured-byte growth guard reads +6,442 B on Wasp's own 30-round mirror match - 157% of the line, already crossed by the deck this project ships, though refresh (4.74 ms worst vs 25 ms), allocation (61% of line), and Undo (47.7 ms vs 150 ms) all hold; the 8.16 gate's four matches commit 15 of 18 dealt faces but assert only the five starting-hand cards, leaving Electroblast (the set's only escalation arm) and Mass Telekinesis committed by no gate match; and schema v23's repeated-Attack `ExcludedTarget` reaches the wire with zero test references and zero corpus fixtures carrying it non-default. **Findings: zero confirmed defects.** One demonstrated performance/resource problem (F-CORE-10, the mis-aimed capture), five architectural growth risks (F-CARD-10 Wasp's content revision did not advance at 8.13 because the composition pin cannot see the Ultimate's triggered-effect reference; F-CARD-11 the shared capability catalog reaches into hero content by type at `SyntheticEffectCapabilities.cs:104`, so each hero edits a Core engine file and re-pins ~23 compatibility artifacts; F-CARD-12 the printed-icon hero selection couples each narrowing to the channel Wasp's cards pair it with, deliberately routed to 8.19 where five heroes' real text can settle the missing combinations; F-CARD-13 six enum vocabularies cross the effect binding as bare integers with no load-time domain check, failing at the table as an empty offer instead of at content load; F-CORE-11 the batch push publishes stop causes for exactly two heroes under positional names, deferred on the named trigger of a mapped hero pushing more than two), and three maintainability/test-quality problems (F-TEST-07 the uncovered v23 wire section; F-TEST-08 the gate's incidental content coverage; F-TEST-09 two Wasp-era relation-vocabulary arms - `RestrictedActorRelation.HeroesAlliedWithSource` and `SourceTeamRelation.AlliedWithSource` - live in production with no declarer and no test, the recorded `unreachable-component` granularity gap recurring). One stylistic observation, no ID, no action. **Preserved strengths were inspected, not assumed**, thirteen recorded - led by the generic Active-effect handler whose no-mode-selector rule is correct and load-bearing, the one-place icon-selection handler, the mechanically inert transcription, the declarable-modification anti-speculation rule with its eleven rulebook-cited exclusions, validator coverage on both content paths including the Ultimate, the single catalog origin, content identity on the wire as contents rather than name, load-time refusal of half-composed content, the data-driven six-hero setup layer, and the genuinely unscripted gate that caught 8.16's two defects. **Gate-blocking: nothing.** Additional pitch-hero inventory (8.17, 8.18a-8.18e) may proceed once this review is approved; three findings (F-CARD-10, F-CARD-13, F-TEST-07) are owed before hero content is implemented, not before inventory begins. **Proposed corrective milestone 8.16b** closes seven findings on seven named checks, each with an observable failing-then-passing transition; F-CARD-12 and F-CORE-11 route to 8.19 as assessment inputs beside the recommendation to register all five heroes' capabilities in one batched milestone rather than five re-pin ceremonies. Scheduling awaits exact user approval, and every closure is mechanical, so no verification milestone is scheduled. **The temporary-structure register row this milestone owed its re-review** was verified claim by claim: four of six claims hold exactly, and the row was corrected in this commit - retirement owner now milestone 8.25 together with `PlaceholderOneVsOneTestConfiguration` (every reachable path runs through it), "Edit Mode fixtures" corrected to production-assembly types used only by fixtures, and the deck's interim role as the reference content for the four standing performance lines recorded until F-CORE-10 is dispositioned; no early retirement recommended. **Validation** T0 per the review-gate rule: living-document validator 127/127 self-tests, 58 governed active leaves, 0 warnings after the ceremony edits; `git diff --check` clean; no source, test, scene, or asset changed; no Unity suite rerun because the 8.16 gate's verified evidence stands and no evidence gap justified one. Full findings, dispositions, the finding-disposition table, and the register re-review live in this milestone's completion commit body. | `b31a8dbb` |

## Milestone 8.18a Inventory and verify pitch hero slot A completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
