## Milestone 8.35p Move a charge before its attack resolves, and wall a card-text attack against its own seat's rewind completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35p | **The NINTH amendment's corrective, and it took three changes rather than the one the diagnosis predicted.** The user, from live multiplayer testing: "Brogan should move for his charge before the attack resolves ... They move as soon as they select, and then they attack." Worker C measured the cause as effect-graph ordering - not a rules defect (targets were already measured from the destination) and not presentation batching (every pause publishes a projection) - and measured the affected set as exactly four playable faces, of which only Brogan's three ask twice. **THE REORDER.** `CreateCharge` becomes `[actor-space, choose-charge-line, move, choose-target, set-attack-target]`: the charge selection asks only where he lands and publishes only `destination`, the Move commits on that answer in the same accepted command, and the unit is named afterwards by an ordinary `choose-target@2` with no declared activity, so immunity still inherits the enclosing Attack. No capability is registered, the persisted schema stays at **v35**, and no wire protocol moves. The showcase `ChargeLineNodes()` follows, making true a summary it had claimed since 8.21. **WHY IT IS A REORDERING**, asserted rather than argued: `ChargeLine_OffersOnlyDestinationsWhoseTargetSetSurvivesTheMove` computes, for every offered destination, the enemy units within 1 of it before the Move and the enemy units within 1 of the hero after it, through the one shared `RosterPresenceQuery`, and asserts set equality - it passes on both sides of the change, which is the point. p.10's stop is unmoved: only destinations with a legal target are offered, and that filter now stands before the Move. **THE DEVIATION, MEASURED.** The plain reorder greyed the charge in exactly the position the card exists for - Mad Dash offered at HEAD in all four staged placements where the only reachable enemy was not already adjacent, and offered in none of them after the reorder alone; offered only where an enemy was ALREADY beside him. These faces bind by card text, so availability runs `EffectOptionFeasibilityQuery.CanCompleteMandatoryAttackTargetBinding`, whose walk judged the new adjacency question from the pre-Move square and reported `Blocked`. The walk now carries `afterAnUntakenMove` and stops JUDGING - never stops walking, so it still reaches the binding - once it crosses a `synthetic-direct-move@3` node, naming the typed Move contract exactly as that walk already names `set-attack-target@1`. It can only suppress a refusal, never manufacture one, which is that query's own standing direction; nothing is offered that cannot bind, because the destination node stands before the Move and is judged normally. Blast radius by grep: the only card-text-bound Attacks holding a Move before their binding are Brogan's three charges and Blink Strike, and Blink Strike has no capability node between its Move and its binding, so its verdict is unchanged - deliberately, per its own declaration that asking twice "would let a player pick a line and then a unit that is not on it". **THE ATTACK WALL, CONFIRMED BY DRIVING BEFORE ANYTHING CHANGED**, which is what the diagnosis demanded and what 8.35f's own lesson requires of a state a walk merely passes through. Driven at HEAD through the real `MatchHost` seat path on the official six-player session: the charge text opened at accepted 2380, both questions were answered with the hero still on `(2, 1)`, the Attack settled at 2384, and the acting seat's rewind was **offered and ACCEPTED** - 2384 back to 2383, taking back the very answer that named the target. `RefreshSeatUndoWatermark` now raises the wall on a standing resolution whose `Target.IsDefined` **and** on the transition where a resolution that was standing is gone; the second clause is not redundant, because naming the victim binds the target and completes the whole p.14 action inside one command, leaving nothing standing to read - a state-only rule was measured doing nothing. Before the target is named the same resolution stands without one, so the committed Move stays rewindable, which is the amendment's own rule. **EVIDENCE.** Three isolated failing-then-passing observations, each against the revision that isolates its own fix, in a `git worktree` at `4bfe6b45`: the reorder (`Expected: Applied But was: NeedsChoice`; `Expected: charge-destination But was: charge-destination-target`), the walk (`Expected: True But was: False`, with the reorder applied and the walk at HEAD - and the other two charge cases then fail too, which is what says the walk change is required by the reorder rather than incidental), and the wall (`Its own seat is published no rewind over a resolved card-text Attack. Expected: False But was: True`, with reorder and walk applied and `MatchHost` at HEAD). **The permanent player-path gate was proved offline in about a second**: a console project compiling `Core/**` plus the real `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.cs` dumped the derived shape set of all **114 faces** before and after, and the diff is EMPTY - the charges keep both board shapes, the space now contributed by `choose-charge-line` and the unit by `choose-target`, so `CapabilityShapes` moved and no row did. No Unity launch was spent on the derivation. Only Brogan's content identity moved (`brogan-8-21-rev-1` -> `brogan-8-35p-rev-1`), with his eighteen `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalog.json` entries re-pinned in the same edit - the 8.35l trap, that fixture being harness-EXCLUDED - and the five other heroes byte-identical, which is the measurement that says the reorder reached his content and nobody else's. **AN ID COLLISION, RECORDED BECAUSE THE EXISTING RULE DID NOT PREVENT IT:** this milestone was built as 8.35o and renumbered before commit. The pre-claim grep over the roadmap AND the tree - the 8.35l lesson - came back clean, because the parallel worker's own 8.35o existed only in a `tmp/` draft at that moment. Grepping proves an id free of committed and uncommitted work; it cannot prove another agent is not about to take it, and only the coordinator or a re-verification after the peer's commits can. **VALIDATION.** Cross-platform harness 1,993/1,993 at HEAD and 1,998/1,998 at the final revision, unblocked all session at about six minutes a run. Complete Unity Edit Mode **2,435 / 2,435** (baseline 2,430; the +5 is this milestone's own, verified per fixture in the results XML) and Play Mode **278 / 278**, unmoved rather than unrun. The session-start baseline, before any edit here, was 1,992 passed / 1 failed / 1,993 total, the single red being the parallel worker's uncommitted lobby work and recorded as inherited. | `6c516a9b` |

## Milestone 8.35q Fill empty seats with spawned test drivers for solo playtesting completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35q | THIRTEENTH amendment: "I find it really hard to test running 6 clients at once. Can you make AI play the game or something so i just play with one client only?" Exposed the existing rehearsal/SixClient machinery's real-lobby, real-HUD, offers-intersected-with-AuthorizedCommands pattern as a shipped, user-facing practice affordance - explicitly NOT an AI opponent, a durable non-goal left unamended. **Host affordance is a lobby control, not a launcher flag** (`PrivateLobbyControl.FillWithTestDrivers`, "FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS"), offered on the host's own screen exactly while a seat is still Open and the match has not started - grounded in 8.35o's own finding that the interactive lobby (`Tools/PlaytestLaunchers/HOST - Start Server.bat`) is the only host path a real solo player actually uses; the raw `-guardsPrivateHost` CLI role stays CI/rehearsal-only. **Reused the rehearsal machinery's pattern rather than its code**: `PrivateOnlineRehearsalDriver.AuthorizedProjectedCommands` (the offers-∩-AuthorizedCommands intersection the amendment names) bumped `internal` and called directly, zero duplication; a new, deliberately separate `PrivateSeatDriver` (new file) carries the shipped behavior - real lobby join, answers whatever it is offered at a configurable 2 s default think-delay - rather than growing the 1184-line CI-only rehearsal driver into two things at once. New CLI vocabulary (`-guardsTestDriver`, `-guardsTestDriverSeat`, optional think-seconds/seed) with endpoint/key carried only through environment variables, the same command-line-secrecy rule the rehearsal driver already follows, independently satisfied rather than borrowed. **Spawning is the host's own real OS child process** (`System.Diagnostics.Process.Start` on its own running executable, `-batchmode -nographics`, a distinct log path per child - several copies of one build sharing a default log path would otherwise fight over it), reached through `ITestDriverProcessLauncher`, a small seam that makes the spawning DECISION Edit-Mode-testable without ever starting a real process; `RealTestDriverProcessLauncher` is production. Killed on session end (`Leave()` + `OnDestroy()` safety net) - verified against the rehearsal runner's own process-management shape, not assumed. **Labeling is deliberately host-screen-only**: `PrivateLobbySeatRow.IsTestDriverSeat` is sourced from the host's own local spawn record, never the roster - putting it on the wire would be the protocol change the amendment says isn't expected; recorded as a deliberate narrowing rather than silently done. No new session-layer member, no protocol change - verified by grep and by the regression completing under the unmoved wire version. **A real conformance defect found and fixed, not routed around**: `PrivateLobbyTests.TheLobbyModel_IsBuiltFromTheSessionContractsAndNothingElse`, a reflection check enumerating `PrivateLobbyModel`'s factory parameter types against a declared allow-list, correctly flagged `ForHost`'s new `ISet<SeatId>` parameter as outside "the session contracts". Fixed by extending the allow-list with a recorded justification (host-local knowledge, reported not decided - the same shape `string feedback` already has), not by weakening the check. **Two live-fire bugs in the bot's own decision logic, found and fixed via a fast offline reproduction rather than repeated 20-minute live cycles**: (1) the Initiative-tie candidate was picked at random per bot rather than deterministically first, so independently-seeded teammates proposed different tied cards with no revision logic to converge them - a disagreement nobody was scripted to resolve, hanging forever; fixed to always pick `Candidates[0]`, matching the proven rehearsal driver's own "ordinary" branch, since a team AGREEMENT is the one family where legality and convergence are not the same property. (2) A pending Minion Battle choice and a standing MinionBattle team agreement can be simultaneously non-null - the agreement is the standing proposal being confirmed, coexisting with a still-changeable underlying selection - and checking the agreement first (as the code originally did) made every bot on the choosing side wait forever on a turn-condition that could only become true once the choice stopped changing, which nothing was left free to do; fixed by reading `PendingMinionBattleChoice.CanConfirm` first, again matching the proven rehearsal driver's exact priority order missed when first simplifying it. Both reproduced in 9-10 seconds each via a throwaway harness probe replaying the exact decision logic against a LOCAL (no network) `IMatchHost`, versus the ~20 minutes a live process-cohort cycle costs per attempt - deleted before commit, per the harness-probe convention. **A near-miss caught before it cost a Unity launch**: a first Play Mode test pressed the real control through an `internal` test seam that would not have compiled in real Unity - `GuardsDigital.Presentation`'s `InternalsVisibleTo` deliberately excludes Play Mode (recorded at 8.30g) - caught by re-reading the actual grant rather than trusting the one-assembly compile probe, which cannot see this class of error; removed, coverage stays at the Edit Mode presenter/model level (no MonoBehaviour lifecycle needed - `PrivateLobbyPresenter` is a POCO) plus the real end-to-end regression below. **Evidence, all measured in an isolated `git worktree` at HEAD `04d2d090`** (the shared tree carried the concurrent event-channel worker's own uncommitted, transiently-uncompiling WIP touching `IMatchReadModel.cs`/`PrivateSessionHost.cs`/`PrivateSessionClient.cs`/`SeatProjection.cs`/`NetworkMatchClient.cs` and more - confirmed by `git status`, none of it this milestone's; the isolated worktree, built by copying `Library` and this milestone's own diff onto a clean HEAD checkout, sidesteps it entirely rather than racing it, the same technique 8.35n's own notes record): the regression itself, `Tools/Invoke-PrivateOnlineRehearsal.ps1 -Topology Solo -FaultPlan None` (new topology; `ValidateSet` extended, `GUARDS_TESTDRIVER_THINK_SECONDS` set on the runner's own environment before launching the host so a truly random-legal match - no strategy, by design - finishes in a bounded window instead of a human-paced one) - **PASSED**: `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME version=1776 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, six distinct `SEAT_CLAIMED` lines from only two launched processes (the scripted seat plus the host), both tracked processes exiting 0 on their own, and a `Get-Process` sweep confirming zero surviving `GuardsDigitalSmoke.exe` after both exited - the host's own proof it killed its five spawned children. **The isolated worktree's own prepared-private-asset gap, closed for full parity rather than footnoted.** A clean `git worktree` checkout carries none of the untracked private board/card/hero-token art the main tree had already prepared locally (`Tools/Prepare-OfficialBoardArtwork.ps1`/`Prepare-HeroTokens.ps1`/`Prepare-CardArtwork.ps1`'s own ignored outputs) - first read as 5 Edit Mode self-skips plus 19 Play Mode failures (every hero's own piece/pointer-target/undo-restart journey, each naming the missing prepared token by name: "Wasp's official circular token is prepared, so the piece must be wearing it rather than the generated prism the fallback draws"), all unrelated to this milestone's diff by their own messages. Copied the three already-prepared private asset directories (`Assets/Resources/Private/{OfficialBoard,Heroes,Cards}`, all untracked, all already present in the main tree, none referenced by this milestone's own diff) from the main tree into the worktree rather than accept a partial or footnoted result. Complete unfiltered Edit Mode **2,451/2,451, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. Complete unfiltered Play Mode **278/278, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, exactly the stated baseline and unmoved, as an Application/Presentation change adding no new journey should leave it. Non-goals honored: no AI opponent, strategy, or difficulty; no wire/protocol change; no in-process second driving path; no change to the CI-only rehearsal driver's scripted cohorts beyond the new `Solo` mode this regression itself exercises. | `d2de7f1a` |

## Milestone 8.35r Publish the seat-public match log and who the table is waiting on completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35r | The TENTH amendment's event channel plus the who-is-awaited fact, gathered into ONE protocol bump as the coordinator's scope ruling required. Three members join `IMatchReadModel`, all **carried, none excused** - the wire-growth ratchet's exclusion floor was already at its limit of 7. `MatchLog` is the seat-public history the amendment asked for, selected per `worker-b/EVENT_LOG_CONTRACT.md` and derived from the retained rule-event log; `DecisionOwner` publishes who the authority is waiting on, which makes the four tray arms milestone 8.35m deferred (levelling hero, defence answerer, respawn chooser, and the acting-hero arm's decision-standing correctness) derivable by every viewer; `UndoMarker` names the standing rewind. **The derivation source was forced, not chosen**: `MatchAggregate.Restore` installs an empty round-event stream while restoring the rule-event log in full, so only the latter survives a snapshot restore - a channel built on round events would come back blank and break the contract's "restore redraws the log for free". **The transport was decided by measurement**: the largest complete projection is 30,575 B against the synchronization sweep's half-bound guard of 32,747, leaving 2,173 B, so the log cannot ride inside the seat record; a first implementation gave it its own message kind and the fault-injection suite falsified that too - a second host frame consumes a gameplay sequence and absorbed the very gap a dropped projection must leave for the client's recovery to see, while the host's per-connection send bookkeeping was silently invalidated by any dropped frame. It now rides inside the seat projection message beside the record, as the whole 256-line window, inheriting the ordered-stream discipline already proven for the projection; all nine reds went green with no fixture edit. Whole projection-and-log frame measured at 47,654 B against a 65,494 B bound, guarded at 80%. **Reconnect-history semantics, recorded**: an arriving seat, a returning seat and one that never left all receive the same bounded recent window, never an archive. **The undo marker is a read-model fact, not a synthetic log entry** - undo appends nothing, so an entry would have to be stored, which would move the snapshot schema and break the channel's derivation invariant, and its sequence would collide with the real event that reuses it after truncation; the recorded limit is that a rewind played past leaves no permanent line. **The blocked-action line names no blocker**: `SkillEffectNodeStopped` translates from a generic `ImpossibleMandatory` outcome with no modification in hand, so naming one would need a second provenance path out of effect execution - milestone 8.35l's offer-side `ActionUnavailability` stays the single channel. Protocol `guards-private-online/5` -> **/6**; **snapshot schema v35 unmoved** (nothing new is persisted). Defect found by re-reading the row table rather than by any run: the Clear row emitted two lines per clear, invisible to every seeded walk because they perform zero Clears. Selection refinements recorded: `Commitment.RevealCompleted` and `Round.MinionBattleSelectionChanged` not carried, and the Move line is a fold over committed steps because `MovementActionCompleted` states neither origin nor destination. Harness 1,998 -> **2,020** (`MatchLogChannelTests` 13, `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests` 9). | 76bff78f |

## Milestone 8.35s Name why a greyed control cannot be taken, in a cursor tooltip completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35s | SIXTH amendment's user-approved cursor-tooltip delivery, the pre-package UX batch's M5. **The de-risk held**: milestone 8.35l's `ActionOffer.Unavailability` was already crossing Core, the wire and the read model into `PrintedActionRegionViewModel.Offer` unread - the whole milestone was reading it and composing words, never a rule re-derived here or a new wire member. New sibling `Presentation/Match/ActionUnavailabilityLabels.cs` (following `NarrationLabels`' own pattern - static methods over already-resolved names, throwing on blank input) holds three shapes: `BlockedBy(hero, card)` composes milestone 8.35l's reason; `NoTarget` names Clear's own offer, which `ClearOptionProjection` stamps unavailable for exactly one reason (`AdjacentTokens.Count == 0`) and no other, verified by reading its own constructor rather than assumed; `Generic` is the recorded absence for every other unstated cause. **The generic word's honesty is the milestone's real finding**: `TurnResolutionAuthority.StampAvailability`'s doc comment says plainly that only the Skill-restriction clause explains itself, and reading the method confirmed the other three - an Attack/Skill/Clear proven empty, an action definition whose card text has no typed composition yet, an actor restriction `ActorMayPerform` does not thread through `ActionUnavailability`, and (milestone 8.29f, measured the most common in ordinary play) the card's other printed action already under way while its sibling resolves - all collapse into the same unstated `Kind == None` with no published bit distinguishing them. Naming any one of them `NO TARGET` would therefore be a guess disguised as a derivation, so the printed-action-region family draws the generic word until a later Core milestone threads a typed reason per clause the way 8.35l threaded one for the Skill clause alone. Same discipline applied to `UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs`'s effect-choice unavailable-mode control, which the pause names no cause for at all. **Two named gaps recorded rather than papered over**: "NOT ENOUGH COINS" has no wired site anywhere in the shipped build - p.15's Level Up auto-purchases whenever affordable (`HeroLevelUpStepRules.TryPurchaseLevel`) and never draws an unaffordable candidate, confirmed by grepping for any other coin-cost gate and finding none; and the SIXTH amendment's own "blocked board targets get the refused-ring treatment" is unreachable from Presentation-only territory as things stand - a barred candidate is *absent* from the offered list by milestone 8.35l's own narrowing rather than drawn-and-refused, `EffectChoiceTargetViewModel`s are built only from that already-narrowed `PendingEffectChoice.Candidates`, and even if a per-candidate exclusion list existed, `ActionOffer.Unavailability` is `None` on every *available* offer by construction (the constructor blanks it whenever `isAvailable` is true) - so there is no reason text to attach to an individually-barred board piece today, a Core/Application-territory gap flagged for a future milestone rather than one this Presentation-only slice could close. **Real content proves the strongest case**: `ActionUnavailabilityTooltipTests.BlockedByRuleModification_NamesTheBlockingHeroAndCard_...` drives the actual Sabina-inside-Magnetic-Dagger's-disk board `SwapAndPlaceRestrictionFeasibilityTests` already proved at Core level, through `RoundActionPresenter.CreateViewModel`, and asserts the composed hover reads `BLOCKED BY WASP — MAGNETIC DAGGER` using the exact hero/card display names read fresh off the same projection (not hardcoded), closing the loop from `TurnResolutionAuthority` through the wire (unchanged, already proven at 8.35l) to the drawn tooltip. A sibling case on 8.35l's *other* fixture (one partner pinned, one free) proves the board half of the story: the narrowed candidate list names only the free minion and invents no ring or reason for the barred one, because the offer stays available and genuinely carries none - the honest reading of the recorded gap above, not a workaround for it. A third, reusing the pre-existing `RoundPresenter_GreysASkillThatCanDoNothing...` fixture (a Skill proven empty, no modification involved), proves the generic-word rule on real content rather than a synthetic offer. **Pre-fix red, isolated**: the vocabulary class kept present and inert, the three composition sites in `MatchFeaturePresenters.cs`/`UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs` reverted to their pre-milestone text; the same focused Edit Mode filter came back 197/200, exactly the three cases naming the new behaviour (`ActionUnavailabilityTooltipTests.BlockedByRuleModification_...`, `ClearPresentationTests.TheGreyedClearControl_...`, `MatchFeaturePresenterTests.RoundPresenter_GreysASkillThatCanDoNothing...`) failing in isolation; the fourth new case (the narrowed-candidate board proof, pre-existing 8.35l behaviour untouched by this revert) stayed green throughout, confirming it tests the right thing. Restored and reconfirmed 200/200. Focused Edit Mode filter: `ActionUnavailabilityTooltipTests` (new, 12 cases), `ClearPresentationTests`, `MatchFeaturePresenterTests`, `MatchHudPresentationTests` - derived by grepping every fixture referencing `PrintedActionRegionViewModel`/`ClearOfferViewModel`/`UnavailableEffectChoiceViewModel`/`CreatePrintedActionRegions`. Play Mode: none run: the changed surface is `AssistiveLabel`/tooltip text only, and the existing Play Mode drivers that touch `PrintedActionRegionViewModel` (`WaspUiPlayerDriver` and siblings) read `.Offer`/`.Invocation` to pick moves, never `.AssistiveLabel` - confirmed by grep - so no Unity-facing path a Play Mode journey could exercise changed, mirroring milestone 8.35h's own precedent for an unreachable-by-journey change. **Recorded limitation, per the coordinator's own instruction**: a parallel worker's uncommitted Core/Application/Presentation-Interaction WIP (simultaneous leveling and related files - `MatchSession.cs`, `MatchSessionProjection.cs`, `HeroLevelUp.cs`, `SystemTransition*.cs`, `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer*.cs`, the `PrivateOnline` protocol files, `Presentation/Interaction/*`, and a new `SimultaneousHeroLevelUpTests.cs`) shared this tree throughout; none of it was read, absorbed, or touched, and no unfiltered suite total is claimed at this revision for that reason, matching milestone 8.35h's own recorded precedent.
